problems go through [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md), not public issues.

## Reporting bugs

Use the bug report form. It asks for the device, ROM, Android version and root manager,
which matter a lot here. Helpful extras:

```sh
su --mount-master -c /data/local/uburoot/bin/rootinfo.sh
```

## Before you write code

Read [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md): it is the architecture guide, and it records why things are
the way they are (mount propagation, uid 9000, the network groups...). Most of them were
learned the hard way on a real device.

Ground rules:

1. **The critical safety rule.** Nothing may run `rm -r` under `/data/local/uburoot`
   except `ubu_safe_rm` in `common.sh`. With `/data` and `/sdcard` bind-mounted inside the
   rootfs, a careless delete wipes the phone. Changes near deletion or unmounting must
   keep `tests/test_reset_guard.sh` and the Docker suite green.
2. **System logic lives in the shell scripts** (`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/`), not
   in Kotlin. They are POSIX `sh` that runs under Android's mksh: no `local`, no
   bashisms, `shellcheck -s sh` clean.
3. **No proprietary Google dependencies** (Play Services, Firebase, Play Core) and no
   analytics. Keep dependencies minimal: no Hilt, Room or Retrofit.
4. **Don't guess flags, APIs or versions.** Check them in the source or the docs, and note
   what you checked in CLAUDE.md.
5. Code, comments, commit messages, docs and the default UI strings
   (`res/values/strings.xml`) are in English. Translations are welcome as
   `values-<lang>/strings.xml`; Brazilian Portuguese (`values-pt-rBR`) is kept complete,
   so add new strings there too.

## Development setup

- JDK 21 with `javac`, plus the Android SDK (compileSdk 37).
- Docker, for the real-mount suite.
- Optional: a rooted arm64 phone with adb.

```sh
./gradlew spotlessApply                     # format Kotlin (ktlint; CI runs spotlessCheck)
./gradlew assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest   # app + unit tests
tests/run-all.sh                            # shellcheck, reset guard, Docker suite
tools/adb-push-scripts.sh                   # push the scripts to a phone
tests/device/run.sh                         # device suite (needs Ubuntu installed)
```

The device suites are described in the [README](README.md#tests).

## Pull requests

- Keep them small and focused, with one topic per PR.
- Use [Conventional Commits](https://www.conventionalcommits.org/) (`feat:`, `fix:`,
  `docs:`, `test:`, `ci:`, `chore:`...).
- Add or update tests with the change: Docker checks for script behavior, unit tests for
  Kotlin, device checks for anything only a phone can show.
- Say how you tested it. If you ran it on a device, name the device, ROM and root manager.
- Update CLAUDE.md when you change a design decision, and CHANGELOG.md under
  *Unreleased*.

UbuRoot is licensed under the [GNU GPL v3.0 or later](LICENSE). Contributions are accepted
under the same license (inbound = outbound): by opening a pull request you agree that your
contribution is licensed under GPL-3.0-or-later. There is no CLA. New source files start
with `SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later`.

```

`README.md`:

```md
# UbuRoot

[![CI](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/actions/workflows/ci.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/actions/workflows/ci.yml)
[![Release](https://img.shields.io/github/v/release/reinanbr/uburoot)](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/releases/latest)
[![License](https://img.shields.io/github/license/reinanbr/uburoot)](LICENSE)

**A full Ubuntu (arm64) on your rooted Android phone, with a real terminal.**

UbuRoot installs the official Ubuntu Base rootfs and runs it in a `chroot` using the
root you already have (Magisk, KernelSU or APatch). This is not proot emulation: programs
run natively on the phone's kernel at full speed, with real `sudo`, `apt`, compilers,
servers and network tools. Your shared storage and Android's own commands (`pm`, `am`,
`cmd`, `settings`...) are available inside Ubuntu.

It targets AOSP/LineageOS-style ROMs and has **no Google dependencies**: no Play
Services, Firebase or analytics. The only network calls are the Ubuntu servers
(`cdimage.ubuntu.com`, `ports.ubuntu.com`) and whatever you run yourself.

> **Status: early (v0.3.0).** Everything planned works: installer, terminal tabs, settings,
> the Android commands and start at boot. It has been tested on one device so far (see [Tested on](#tested-on)). Expect rough edges, and read
> the [safety warning](#safety-warning) before you use it.

## Download

| | File | |
|---|---|---|
| **App (APK)** | [uburoot.apk](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/releases/latest/download/uburoot.apk) | install it like any app |
| **Root module (zip)** | [uburoot-module.zip](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/releases/latest/download/uburoot-module.zip) | Magisk / KernelSU / APatch → Modules → *Install from storage* |
| Checksums | [SHA256SUMS](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/releases/latest/download/SHA256SUMS) | |

Both links always point to the latest release. They are **the same file** under two
names, so install either one. All versions and release notes are on the
[releases page](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/releases). See
[Install](#install) for the details.

## Screenshots

<p>
  <img src="fastlane/metadata/android/en-US/images/phoneScreenshots/1_setup.png" width="240" alt="Setup: root manager, busybox, architecture, free space and namespace, plus the user form">
  <img src="fastlane/metadata/android/en-US/images/phoneScreenshots/2_installing.png" width="240" alt="Setting up Ubuntu with the live log">
</p>
<p>
  <img src="fastlane/metadata/android/en-US/images/phoneScreenshots/3_terminal.png" width="490" alt="Terminal: Ubuntu 26.04.1 LTS on the phone, with gcc, Python, Node, Go, Rust, Java and git">
  <img src="fastlane/metadata/android/en-US/images/phoneScreenshots/4_android.png" width="490" alt="Android commands inside Ubuntu: getprop, pm, cmd wifi, settings; /sdcard; /system is read-only">
</p>

*Setup, installing, the terminal with a development toolchain, and Android's own commands
from Ubuntu. The UI is in English and follows the system language; these screenshots
come from a phone set to Brazilian Portuguese.*

## Features

Available now:

- **Root check**: detects Magisk, KernelSU or APatch, makes sure commands run in
  Android's global mount namespace, so every session sees the same mounts, and that the
  root is **full root** (every capability, no seccomp). A restricted root (e.g. a
  KernelSU App Profile with fewer capabilities) is refused with the reason.
- **Installer**: finds the newest Ubuntu LTS automatically (currently 26.04.1), downloads
  it (resumable, with a wake lock so Android doesn't kill it), checks it against
  Ubuntu's `SHA256SUMS`, then sets up DNS, apt sources, `sudo` and your user. Under
  *Advanced*: an APT mirror, or an **offline install** from a `.tar.gz`/`.tar.xz` you
  pick (optionally with its SHA256). Installs from older UbuRoot versions are upgraded
  on start.
- **Diagnostics**: Settings → *Run diagnostics* checks root, the kernel (features, module
  signing, BTF, tracing, restrictions), the hardware (GPU, audio, cameras, sensors, USB,
  Wi-Fi, NFC... and which Android process holds each device), storage, mounts and the
  network. It changes nothing, and the report you share hides IP addresses, names and
  serial numbers unless you include them.
- **Kernel access** (opt-in, Settings → *Kernel access*): root in Ubuntu gets the kernel's
  own interfaces (configfs, debugfs, tracefs, BPF, cgroups, pstore, SELinux), the ROM's
  kernel modules, and `uburoot-kernel` / `uburoot-kmod` / `uburoot-kbuild` for kernel
  settings, modules, SELinux and out-of-tree builds. Every change made with them is
  recorded and undone by *Stop all*, *Revert changes* or a reboot. Menu → *Kernel* shows
  the state and the kernel logs. See [docs/KERNEL.md](docs/KERNEL.md).
- **Hardware access** (opt-in, Settings → *Hardware access*): `uburoot-hw` shows every
  hardware class and who uses it, gives Ubuntu users the device groups (GPU, input, USB,
  serial...), takes Wi-Fi over from Android, sets CPU limits, LEDs and a wake lock.
  Changes are recorded and undone by *Stop all*; thermal, charger and battery settings
  stay read-only. Menu → *Hardware*. See [docs/HARDWARE.md](docs/HARDWARE.md).
- **Terminal**: a real PTY with job control, 256 colors and UTF-8 (Termux's terminal
  view).
- **Android integration**: `/sdcard`, `/data` and the system partitions are available
  inside Ubuntu. `am`, `pm`, `cmd`, `settings`, `dumpsys`, `getprop`, `wm`, `input`,
  `content`, `svc` and `appops` run Android's own binaries; `android <command>` runs any
  other one.
- **One file, three install methods**: the APK is also a root-manager module and a
  Magisk recovery zip (see [Install](#install)).
- **Sessions in tabs**: `+` opens a session as your user, the menu also opens a root
  session. A foreground service keeps them running in the background; its notification
  shows how many are running and offers *New session*, *Stop all* (closes everything and
  releases every mount) and an optional wake lock for long jobs with the screen off.
- **Extra keys**: ESC, CTRL, ALT, TAB, arrows, HOME/END, PgUp/PgDn, `/ - | ~` (CTRL and
  ALT stay pressed for the next key; hold a key to repeat it).
- **Terminal comforts**: pinch to zoom (remembered), themes (dark, light, Solarized dark
  and light), copy and paste, tap a link to open it, and hardware keyboard shortcuts:
  Ctrl+Shift+C / V to copy / paste, Ctrl+Shift+T for a new tab.
- **Settings**: default user, startup command, `/sdcard` and Android-integration mounts,
  DNS servers, APT mirror, *Repair mounts*, and *Reset Ubuntu*, which asks you to type a
  confirmation word and never deletes anything while something is still mounted.

- **Talk to Android from Ubuntu**:

  | Command | Does |
  |---------|------|
  | `uburoot-notify TITLE [TEXT]` | shows an Android notification (e.g. `make; uburoot-notify "build done"`) |
  | `uburoot-toast TEXT` | shows a short message |
  | `uburoot-vibrate [MS]` | vibrates (default 300 ms) |
  | `uburoot-clip [TEXT]` | copies TEXT, or its input (`git log -1 \| uburoot-clip`), to Android's clipboard |
  | `uburoot-open FILE\|URL`, `xdg-open` | opens a file or a link in an Android app |

  They work in the background too. Files are read with your own permissions, so you can
  only open what you can read.
- **Open in UbuRoot**: share a file from any app to open a session in its folder. Files
  outside the phone's shared storage are copied to `~/Shared` first.

- **Start at boot** (needs the module, see [Install](#install)): Settings → *Start Ubuntu
  at boot* mounts Ubuntu when the phone starts and runs, as root, every executable script
  in `/etc/uburoot-boot.d/` in name order. Names may only use letters, digits, `_` and
  `-`, like `run-parts`. For example, `/etc/uburoot-boot.d/10-sshd`:

  ```sh
  #!/bin/sh
  mkdir -p /run/sshd
  exec /usr/sbin/sshd -p 8022   # sshd forks into the background by itself
  ```

  Each script gets 60 seconds (`BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT` in `uburoot.conf`). One still
  running after that is left running and the next one starts. When `/sdcard` is mounted,
  the start waits for the first unlock, because shared storage stays encrypted until
  then. The log goes to `/data/local/uburoot/boot.log`. *Run boot scripts now* in
  Settings does the same without a reboot, e.g. after *Stop all*, which also stops them.

## Requirements

- An **arm64** (`arm64-v8a`) phone with **Android 8.0+**.
- **Root** with Magisk, KernelSU or APatch. Their busybox is used for the mounts.
- About **3 GB free** in `/data` for the install. It grows with what you install, and a
  full development setup takes a few GB more.
- Internet access to download Ubuntu.

### Tested on

| Device | ROM | Root | Result |
|--------|-----|------|--------|
| Samsung Galaxy S9 (SM-G9600) | LineageOS 19.1 (Android 12) | Magisk 31.1 | all device suites pass |

KernelSU, APatch and Android 13+ are supported in the code but **not tested on a device
yet**. How the three root managers differ (and what UbuRoot does about it) is in
[docs/ROOT_IMPLEMENTATIONS.md](docs/ROOT_IMPLEMENTATIONS.md). Reports are very welcome:
[docs/COMPATIBILITY.md](docs/COMPATIBILITY.md) has the device table and a testing
checklist. Please attach the diagnostics report (Settings → *Run diagnostics* → Share).

## Install

Get the files from [Download](#download) (or any version from
[Releases](https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/releases)). The `.apk` and the
`-module.zip` are **the same file**, published twice because some root managers' file
pickers only list `.zip`.

Use one of these:

| Method | Works with | What happens |
|--------|-----------|--------------|
| Install the `.apk` | any root | a normal app install |
| Root manager → Modules → *Install from storage* (`-module.zip`) | Magisk, KernelSU, APatch | installs the module and the app right away |
| Flash the `-module.zip` in a custom recovery (TWRP) | **Magisk only** | installs the module; the app is installed on the next boot |

The module installs the app and, if *Start Ubuntu at boot* is on, starts Ubuntu at boot.
Removing the module never deletes Ubuntu. Recovery flashing is implemented but not
tested yet. KernelSU and APatch modules can't be flashed from
recovery (their docs say so), and the installer stops with a message.

Optional: verify the release signature.

```sh
apksigner verify --print-certs uburoot.apk
# Signer #1 certificate SHA-256 digest:
# b2fbab8052e8bc9ee7cb01bc26f309ce972d16fdda34a52bdb15b2628d2ab53d
```

## First run

1. Open UbuRoot and allow root access when your root manager asks.
2. The app shows what it found (root manager, busybox, architecture, free space,
   namespace).
3. Choose a user name (lowercase, e.g. `ana`), an optional password and whether `sudo`
   should ask for it.
4. Tap install. The app downloads about 35 MB, extracts Ubuntu to
   `/data/local/uburoot/rootfs`, configures it (this runs `apt-get update`) and opens
   the terminal as your user.

If the app says there is no root but you never saw a prompt, open your root manager's
superuser list and allow UbuRoot by hand. On the test phone, Magisk once rejected the app
without showing its prompt.

## Using it

It's a normal Ubuntu: `sudo apt install ...` works.

```sh
# a typical development setup
sudo apt install build-essential cmake git gdb python3-venv python3-pip \
    nodejs npm golang-go rustc cargo default-jdk-headless openssh-server tmux
```

- **Files**: `/sdcard` is your phone's shared storage. Files you create there belong to
  the group `media_rw`, so Android apps can still read and write them. The Gallery may
  need a media rescan to show new files.
- **Android commands**: `pm list packages`, `cmd wifi status`, `getprop`... Some of them
  (`settings`, `dumpsys`, `am get-current-user`) need Android permissions that only root
  has, so run them with `sudo`.
- **No systemd**: Android's `init` is PID 1, so services don't start by themselves, and
  package installs never auto-start daemons. Start them by hand. For example, an SSH
  server for VS Code Remote or `scp`:

  ```sh
  sudo mkdir -p /run/sshd && sudo ssh-keygen -A
  sudo /usr/sbin/sshd -p 8022        # listens on every interface: use keys, not passwords
  ```

  Then, from your computer: `ssh -p 8022 <user>@<phone-ip>`.
- **File watchers**: UbuRoot raises `fs.inotify.max_user_watches` to 524288 (Android's
  default, 8192, is too low for VS Code and webpack). This is a kernel-wide setting that
  is only ever raised, and a reboot resets it.
- **Network tools**: `nmap`, `tcpdump`, `scapy`, `iw`... work with `sudo`. Your user
  can't capture packets or change the network without `sudo`; see [Security
  model](#security-model).
- **iptables** inside Ubuntu edits the **phone's** firewall. UbuRoot switches Ubuntu to
  `iptables-legacy`, which is the backend Android uses.
- **scapy**: Android keeps its default route in per-network routing tables, which
  confuses scapy's routing. Fix scapy's own table (not the kernel's) first:

  ```python
  from scapy.all import conf
  gw = "192.168.1.1"  # from: ip route show table all | grep '^default via'
  conf.route.routes = [r for r in conf.route.routes
                       if r[4] != "0.0.0.0" and not (r[0] == 0 and r[1] == 0)]
  conf.route.invalidate_cache()
  conf.route.add(net="0.0.0.0/0", gw=gw, dev="wlan0")
  ```
- **Wi-Fi monitor mode** depends on your phone's Wi-Fi driver. Run `iw phy` to see the
  supported interface modes. The test phone (Qualcomm) has none.

## Safety warning

> **Never delete `/data/local/uburoot/rootfs` yourself.**
>
> While Ubuntu is mounted, the phone's real `/data` and `/sdcard` are *inside* that
> folder. Deleting it with `rm -rf` or a file manager would **erase your phone's data
> and photos**.

To remove Ubuntu, use the reset script. It stops Ubuntu's processes, unmounts
everything, and refuses to delete anything if a single mount is left:

```sh
su --mount-master -c '/data/local/uburoot/bin/reset.sh --yes'
```

Then uninstall the app, and remove the module in your root manager if you installed it.
The few KB of scripts and config left in `/data/local/uburoot` are harmless.

### Security model

A chroot isolates the file system and nothing else. Ubuntu shares the kernel, the network
and the processes with Android:

- **Root inside Ubuntu is root on your phone.** It can read every app's data, change the
  network or break Android. Use `sudo` as carefully as you would `su`.
- Your user gets **uid 9000**, not Ubuntu's usual 1000. On Android, uid 1000 is the
  `system` user (system_server), and uids from 10000 up are apps, whose network Android
  blocks in some power-saving modes. 9000 is unused by Android and not an app uid.
- Your user is in `aid_inet` (network access) and `aid_media_rw` (shared storage). It is
  **not** in `aid_net_raw`/`aid_net_admin`: on Android kernels those groups grant raw
  sockets and network administration. A user in them could capture all of the phone's
  traffic without `sudo`. If you want that anyway:
  `sudo usermod -aG aid_net_raw,aid_net_admin <user>`.
- `/system`, `/vendor` and `/apex` are mounted read-only, even for root.
- With **Kernel access** on, root in Ubuntu also controls the kernel: settings, modules,
  SELinux, USB gadget, tracing. Changes made with `uburoot-kernel` are undone by *Stop
  all*; changes made by hand last until the next reboot.
- With **Hardware access** on, root in Ubuntu can hand the phone's devices to Ubuntu
  users and turn Android's Wi-Fi off. Changes made with `uburoot-hw` are undone by *Stop
  all*.

## How it works

The app is small. The real work is done by POSIX shell scripts in
[`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/`](app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin), which the app
copies to `/data/local/uburoot/bin/` and runs with `su --mount-master`. They can be used
and tested without the app.

| Script | Does |
|--------|------|
| `install.sh` | checks space and architecture, verifies the SHA256, extracts the tarball |
| `setup.sh` | DNS, hosts, apt sources, groups, `sudo`, the user |
| `mount.sh` | mounts `proc`, `sys`, `/dev`, `/dev/pts`, `/dev/shm`, `/run`, `/sdcard`, `/data` and the Android partitions; safe to run again |
| `enter.sh` | mounts, then enters Ubuntu with a clean environment as root or as the user |
| `umount.sh` | unmounts everything, deepest first |
| `reset.sh` | the only path that deletes the rootfs (see the safety warning) |
| `android-env` | captures Android's environment and creates the wrappers for its commands |

Mount design, in short: the rootfs is bind-mounted onto itself and made *private*, and
every mount inside it is made *slave*. Changes on the Android side (like APEX updates)
still show up in Ubuntu, but nothing done in Ubuntu propagates back. Unmounting Ubuntu's
copy of `/apex` or `/system` can never unmount the real ones. Mounts are not persistent:
opening a terminal re-creates whatever is missing, e.g. after a reboot.

[CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) has the full architecture, every design decision and what was
verified on the device.

## Build from source

You need a JDK 21 (with `javac`) and the Android SDK (compileSdk 37).

```sh
./gradlew assembleDebug testDebugUnitTest
# app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
```

Release builds are signed only when `UBUROOT_KEYSTORE`, `UBUROOT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD`,
`UBUROOT_KEY_ALIAS` and `UBUROOT_KEY_PASSWORD` are set in the environment; otherwise the
release APK is unsigned. Keystores never go into the repository.

### Tests

| Command | Needs | Covers |
|---------|-------|--------|
| `tests/run-all.sh` | Docker | shellcheck, the reset safety guard, a real-mount suite in a privileged container (install from `.tar.gz`/`.tar.xz` → setup with real apt → migrations, mirror → enter → diagnostics → umount → reset) |
| `tests/device/run.sh [--reset]` | rooted phone over adb, Ubuntu installed | full root and the diagnostics report, real mounts and propagation, network, wrappers, a sabotaged reset, umount back to the original state |
| `tests/device/session-test.sh` | run it inside the app's terminal | tty and job control, sudo, network, storage, read-only partitions, package integrity |
| `tests/device/advanced-test.sh` | run inside Ubuntu as root, dev packages installed | network and Wi-Fi tools, compilers and runtimes, debuggers, sshd |

The [CI](.github/workflows/ci.yml) runs the first row plus the build and unit tests on
every push. Tags `v*` publish a signed release.

## FAQ

**Why chroot and not proot (like Termux's proot-distro)?**
proot works without root but intercepts every system call, which is slow and doesn't
fully emulate the kernel. A chroot runs at native speed with real root, real mounts and
raw sockets, at the cost of needing root.

**Does it survive a reboot?**
Ubuntu itself, yes: it lives in `/data/local/uburoot`. The mounts don't, and they are
re-created the next time you open the terminal, or at boot with *Start Ubuntu at boot*
and the module, which can also start services (see [Features](#features)).

**Can I run Docker or systemd services?**
Not currently. There is no systemd, and container runtimes are untested.

**Does it work without Google Play Services?**
Yes. It was built for de-Googled ROMs.

**Which Ubuntu version do I get?**
The newest LTS, discovered at install time from `cdimage.ubuntu.com`. Nothing is
hardcoded.

## Privacy and F-Droid

- No analytics, tracking or ads, and no proprietary libraries (no Google Play Services,
  Firebase or Play Core).
- The app itself only talks to `cdimage.ubuntu.com`: the release listing, `SHA256SUMS`
  and the Ubuntu Base download. Everything else is what you run inside Ubuntu (for
  example, `apt` uses `ports.ubuntu.com`, set up as Ubuntu's normal package source).
- **Anti-features: none apply.**
  - *NonFreeNet* doesn't apply: Ubuntu Base is free software, downloaded once from
    Canonical's public image server, which is not a non-free network service.
  - *NonFreeDep* / *NonFreeAdd* / *Tracking* / *Ads*: none.
  - Root is required. That's not an anti-feature, but the app does nothing without it.
- Release builds come from the source by CI and are signed with the key whose
  fingerprint is listed in [Install](#install).

`SECURITY.md`:

```md
# Security policy

UbuRoot runs as root and bind-mounts the phone's `/data` and `/sdcard` into a chroot,
so bugs here can destroy user data or break Android. Security reports are taken
seriously.

## Supported versions

Only the latest release gets fixes.

## Reporting a vulnerability

**Please do not open a public issue.** Report privately through GitHub:

<https://github.com/reinanbr/uburoot/security/advisories/new>

Include what you can of:

- the UbuRoot version (or commit), device, ROM, Android version, and root manager with its
  version;
- what happens and what you expected;
- steps or a script to reproduce it.

You should get an answer within a week. Once a fix is released, the advisory is published
with credit to you, unless you prefer not to be named.

## What counts

Examples of what we want to hear about:

- any way for UbuRoot to delete or corrupt data outside the rootfs, e.g. a path where
  `reset.sh` or anything else removes files while `/data` or `/sdcard` is still mounted
  inside the rootfs;
- mounts leaking back into Android: a `umount` or mount change inside the chroot that
  affects Android's own mounts (`/apex`, `/system`, ...);
- privilege escalation from the chroot's non-root user to root, or to Android
  permissions it should not have;
- code execution through the app's config file, scripts, downloads or intents;
- a release APK whose signature does not match the published certificate.

Out of scope: what root inside the chroot can do. Root in Ubuntu is root on the phone by
design (see the security model in the [README](README.md#security-model)).

Release APKs are signed with the certificate whose SHA-256 digest is
`b2fbab8052e8bc9ee7cb01bc26f309ce972d16fdda34a52bdb15b2628d2ab53d`.

```

`app/build.gradle.kts`:

```kts
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
plugins {
    alias(libs.plugins.android.application)
    alias(libs.plugins.kotlin.compose)
}

val appVersionName = "0.3.0"
val appVersionCode = 4

android {
    namespace = "com.reinanbr.uburoot"
    compileSdk = 37

    defaultConfig {
        applicationId = "com.reinanbr.uburoot"
        minSdk = 26
        targetSdk = 37
        versionCode = appVersionCode
        versionName = appVersionName
        ndk {
            // The chroot is arm64 only; drop the other ABIs Termux ships.
            abiFilters += "arm64-v8a"
        }
    }

    // No dependency metadata block in the APK signing block: it is encrypted
    // with a Google key, differs on every build (so two builds of one commit
    // never match byte for byte) and F-Droid flags it.
    dependenciesInfo {
        includeInApk = false
        includeInBundle = false
    }

    // Release signing comes from the environment (CI secrets), never from the
    // repo. Unless all four variables are set, the release APK is left unsigned
    // (and CI's release job refuses to publish it).
    fun env(name: String) = providers.environmentVariable(name).orNull?.takeIf { it.isNotEmpty() }
    val keystore = env("UBUROOT_KEYSTORE")?.let(::file)?.takeIf { it.isFile }
    val storePass = env("UBUROOT_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD")
    val alias = env("UBUROOT_KEY_ALIAS")
    val keyPass = env("UBUROOT_KEY_PASSWORD")
    if (keystore != null && storePass != null && alias != null && keyPass != null) {
        signingConfigs {
            create("release") {
                storeFile = keystore
                storePassword = storePass
                keyAlias = alias
                keyPassword = keyPass
            }
        }
    }

    buildTypes {
        release {
            signingConfig = signingConfigs.findByName("release")
            isMinifyEnabled = true
            isShrinkResources = true
            proguardFiles(getDefaultProguardFile("proguard-android-optimize.txt"), "proguard-rules.pro")
        }
    }

    compileOptions {
        sourceCompatibility = JavaVersion.VERSION_17
        targetCompatibility = JavaVersion.VERSION_17
    }

    buildFeatures {
        compose = true
    }
}

dependencies {
    implementation(libs.androidx.core.ktx)
    implementation(libs.androidx.activity.compose)
    implementation(libs.androidx.lifecycle.viewmodel.compose)
    implementation(libs.androidx.lifecycle.runtime.compose)
    implementation(platform(libs.compose.bom))
    implementation(libs.compose.ui)
    implementation(libs.compose.material3)
    implementation(libs.compose.ui.tooling.preview)
    debugImplementation(libs.compose.ui.tooling)
    implementation(libs.kotlinx.coroutines.android)
    implementation(libs.termux.terminal.view)
    implementation(libs.termux.terminal.emulator)
    implementation(libs.libsu.core)

    testImplementation(libs.junit)
}

/**
 * The APK is also a Magisk/KernelSU/APatch module and a Magisk recovery zip:
 * src/module/ (module.prop, customize.sh, service.sh, META-INF/com/google/...)
 * goes to the APK root as Java resources, so it is inside the signed APK.
 */
abstract class ModuleFilesTask : DefaultTask() {
    @get:InputDirectory
    abstract val source: DirectoryProperty

    @get:Input
    abstract val versionName: Property<String>

    @get:Input
    abstract val versionCode: Property<Int>

    @get:OutputDirectory
    abstract val output: DirectoryProperty

    @TaskAction
    fun run() {
        val out = output.get().asFile
        out.deleteRecursively()
        source.get().asFile.copyRecursively(out)
        val prop = out.resolve("module.prop")
        prop.writeText(
            prop.readText()
                .replace("@VERSION_NAME@", versionName.get())
                .replace("@VERSION_CODE@", versionCode.get().toString()),
        )
    }
}

/** LICENSE, NOTICE and LICENSES/ go into the APK's assets/licenses/ for the About screen. */
abstract class LicenseAssetsTask : DefaultTask() {
    @get:InputFiles
    abstract val sources: ConfigurableFileCollection

    @get:OutputDirectory
    abstract val output: DirectoryProperty

    @TaskAction
    fun run() {
        val out = output.get().asFile.resolve("licenses")
        out.deleteRecursively()
        out.mkdirs()
        for (f in sources.files) {
            val name = if (f.name == "LICENSE") "GPL-3.0-or-later.txt" else f.name
            f.copyTo(out.resolve(name))
        }
    }
}

androidComponents {
    onVariants { variant ->
        val licenses = tasks.register<LicenseAssetsTask>("${variant.name}LicenseAssets") {
            sources.from(
                rootProject.file("LICENSE"),
                rootProject.file("NOTICE"),
                rootProject.file("LICENSES/Apache-2.0.txt"),
            )
        }
        variant.sources.assets?.addGeneratedSourceDirectory(licenses, LicenseAssetsTask::output)
        val task = tasks.register<ModuleFilesTask>("${variant.name}ModuleFiles") {
            source.set(layout.projectDirectory.dir("src/module"))
            versionName.set(appVersionName)
            versionCode.set(appVersionCode)
        }
        variant.sources.resources?.addGeneratedSourceDirectory(task, ModuleFilesTask::output)
    }
}

```

    <!-- Only for cdimage.ubuntu.com (rootfs download). -->
    <uses-permission android:name="android.permission.INTERNET" />
    <!-- SessionService keeps the terminal sessions alive in the background. -->
    <uses-permission android:name="android.permission.FOREGROUND_SERVICE" />
    <uses-permission android:name="android.permission.FOREGROUND_SERVICE_SPECIAL_USE" />
    <uses-permission android:name="android.permission.POST_NOTIFICATIONS" />

`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/android-env`:

```
#!/system/bin/sh
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Capture the Android runtime environment into $ROOTFS/etc/android.env and
# (re)generate wrappers in $ROOTFS/usr/local/bin that run Android binaries
# (am, pm, cmd, settings, dumpsys, getprop, android <any>) with that env.
# Runs on the Android side (outside the chroot).
# Usage: android-env [--if-needed]
#   --if-needed  only capture when android.env is missing or the build
#                fingerprint changed (OTA)
set -u
UBU_BIN_DIR=$(cd -P -- "$(dirname -- "$0")" && pwd -P)
# shellcheck source=common.sh
. "$UBU_BIN_DIR/common.sh"

ubu_require_root
ubu_init

R=$ROOTFS
ENV_FILE="$R/etc/android.env"
WRAP_DIR="$R/usr/local/bin"
# Binder shell commands get a wrapper each (only those present on the device).
WRAPPERS="am pm cmd settings dumpsys getprop wm input content svc appops"
# Bump when the generated wrappers change, so --if-needed regenerates them.
WRAP_VERSION=5
# Non-binder Android tools that keep the terminal (perf for this kernel).
TTY_WRAPPERS="simpleperf"
RUNNER_DIR="$R/usr/local/lib/uburoot"

VARS="ANDROID_ROOT ANDROID_DATA ANDROID_ART_ROOT ANDROID_I18N_ROOT ANDROID_TZDATA_ROOT
ANDROID_RUNTIME_ROOT ANDROID_ASSETS ANDROID_STORAGE EXTERNAL_STORAGE ASEC_MOUNTPOINT
BOOTCLASSPATH DEX2OATBOOTCLASSPATH SYSTEMSERVERCLASSPATH STANDALONE_SYSTEMSERVER_JARS"

ANDROID_PATH=/product/bin:/apex/com.android.runtime/bin:/apex/com.android.art/bin:/system_ext/bin:/system/bin:/system/xbin:/odm/bin:/vendor/bin:/vendor/xbin

# App bridge (Phase 4): uburoot-notify/toast/vibrate/clip/open and xdg-open.
# A chroot user's uid holds no Android permission, so `am broadcast` to the
# app's signature-protected receiver must come from root: the commands go
# through one root helper that sudo allows to everyone (see chroot-send.sh).
# Installed on every run (cheap), so script updates arrive without a
# WRAP_VERSION bump.
ubu_install_file() { # SRC DEST MODE
    cp -f "$1" "$2.tmp" && chown 0:0 "$2.tmp" && chmod "$3" "$2.tmp" && mv -f "$2.tmp" "$2"
}
ubu_install_bridge() {
    mkdir -p "$RUNNER_DIR" "$WRAP_DIR" || return 1
    ubu_install_file "$UBU_BIN_DIR/chroot-send.sh" "$RUNNER_DIR/uburoot-send" 755 || return 1
    ubu_install_file "$UBU_BIN_DIR/chroot-cmd.sh" "$RUNNER_DIR/uburoot-cmd" 755 || return 1
    for _c in uburoot-notify uburoot-toast uburoot-vibrate uburoot-clip uburoot-open xdg-open; do
        ln -sf ../lib/uburoot/uburoot-cmd "$WRAP_DIR/$_c" || return 1
    done
    [ -d "$R/etc/sudoers.d" ] || return 0
    _sf="$R/etc/sudoers.d/80-uburoot"
    printf '%s\n' \
        '# Written by UbuRoot android-env: every user may send uburoot-* commands' \
        '# to the Android app. The helper reads files as the calling user.' \
        'ALL ALL=(root) NOPASSWD: /usr/local/lib/uburoot/uburoot-send' >"$_sf.tmp" || return 1
    chmod 440 "$_sf.tmp" && mv -f "$_sf.tmp" "$_sf"
}

_fp=$(getprop ro.build.fingerprint 2>/dev/null) || _fp=""

if [ "${1:-}" = --if-needed ] && [ -s "$ENV_FILE" ]; then
    if grep -qxF "# fingerprint=$_fp" "$ENV_FILE" 2>/dev/null &&
        grep -qxF "# wrappers=$WRAP_VERSION" "$ENV_FILE" 2>/dev/null; then
        ubu_install_bridge || ubu_warn "cannot install the uburoot-* commands"
        exit 0
    fi
fi

# Look a variable up in: current env, derive_classpath output, init.environ.rc.
ubu_env_lookup() {
    _val=$(printenv "$1" 2>/dev/null) || _val=""
    if [ -n "$_val" ]; then
        printf '%s' "$_val"
        return 0
    fi
    for _f in /data/system/environ/classpath /init.environ.rc /system/etc/init/hw/init.environ.rc; do
        [ -r "$_f" ] || continue
        _val=$(bb awk -v n="$1" '$1 == "export" && $2 == n { print $3; exit }' "$_f")
        if [ -n "$_val" ]; then
            printf '%s' "$_val"
            return 0
        fi
    done
    return 1
}

mkdir -p "$R/etc" "$WRAP_DIR" || ubu_die "cannot create $R/etc or $WRAP_DIR"
_tmp="$ENV_FILE.tmp.$$"
{
    printf '# Generated by UbuRoot android-env on the Android side. Do not edit.\n'
    printf '# fingerprint=%s\n' "$_fp"
    printf '# wrappers=%s\n' "$WRAP_VERSION"
    printf "export PATH='%s'\n" "$ANDROID_PATH"
    for _v in $VARS; do
        if _val=$(ubu_env_lookup "$_v"); then
            # Values are colon-separated paths; strip quotes defensively.
            _val=$(printf '%s' "$_val" | tr -d "'")
            printf "export %s='%s'\n" "$_v" "$_val"
        fi
    done
} >"$_tmp" || ubu_die "cannot write $_tmp"
grep -q '^export BOOTCLASSPATH=' "$_tmp" || ubu_warn "BOOTCLASSPATH not found; am/pm may not work"
if ! { chmod 644 "$_tmp" && mv -f "$_tmp" "$ENV_FILE"; }; then
    ubu_die "cannot install $ENV_FILE"
fi

mkdir -p "$RUNNER_DIR" || ubu_die "cannot create $RUNNER_DIR"
# Runner executed by Android's /system/bin/sh (mksh) inside the chroot:
#   android-run --pipes|--tty BINARY [ARGS...]
cat >"$RUNNER_DIR/android-run.tmp" <<'EOF'
# Generated by UbuRoot android-env. Interpreted by /system/bin/sh (mksh).
_mode=$1
shift
. /etc/android.env || exit 127
if [ "$_mode" = --pipes ]; then
    # Binder shell commands (cmd, am, pm, settings, dumpsys...) hand our
    # stdin/stdout/stderr to the service, and SELinux does not let
    # system_server use the terminal app's pty (untrusted_app_all_devpts):
    # the whole transaction fails. Give them pipes instead of the tty;
    # pipefail keeps the command's exit status.
    [ -t 0 ] && exec </dev/null
    if [ -t 1 ] || [ -t 2 ]; then
        set -o pipefail
        { "$@" 2>&1 1>&3 3>&- | /system/bin/cat >&2; } 3>&1 | /system/bin/cat
        exit
    fi
fi
exec "$@"
EOF
if ! { chmod 644 "$RUNNER_DIR/android-run.tmp" && mv -f "$RUNNER_DIR/android-run.tmp" "$RUNNER_DIR/android-run"; }; then
    ubu_die "cannot install $RUNNER_DIR/android-run"
fi

# ubu_write_wrapper NAME ANDROID_BINARY [--pipes|--tty]
ubu_write_wrapper() {
    _w="$WRAP_DIR/$1"
    _mode=${3:---pipes}
    cat >"$_w.tmp" <<EOF
#!/bin/sh
# Generated by UbuRoot android-env: run $2 with the Android environment.
if [ ! -x /system/bin/sh ] || [ ! -r /etc/android.env ]; then
    echo "uburoot: Android integration is off (no /system in the chroot)" >&2
    exit 127
fi
exec /usr/bin/env -i \${TERM:+TERM=\$TERM} /system/bin/sh /usr/local/lib/uburoot/android-run $_mode $2 "\$@"
EOF
    chmod 755 "$_w.tmp" && mv -f "$_w.tmp" "$_w"
}

_wrapped=""
for _c in $WRAPPERS; do
    [ -x "/system/bin/$_c" ] || continue
    ubu_write_wrapper "$_c" "/system/bin/$_c" || ubu_warn "wrapper $_c failed"
    _wrapped="${_wrapped:+$_wrapped|}$_c"
done

for _c in $TTY_WRAPPERS; do
    [ -x "/system/bin/$_c" ] || continue
    ubu_write_wrapper "$_c" "/system/bin/$_c" --tty || ubu_warn "wrapper $_c failed"
done

# Generic runner: android <cmd> [args...]  (e.g. android logcat -d). Keeps the
# real tty for interactive programs (android sh, top); binder tools get pipes.
cat >"$WRAP_DIR/android.tmp" <<EOF
#!/bin/sh
# Generated by UbuRoot android-env: run any Android binary with the Android env.
if [ \$# -eq 0 ]; then
    echo "usage: android <command> [args...]   (runs /system/bin/<command> by default)" >&2
    exit 2
fi
if [ ! -x /system/bin/sh ] || [ ! -r /etc/android.env ]; then
    echo "uburoot: Android integration is off (no /system in the chroot)" >&2
    exit 127
fi
cmd=\$1
shift
mode=--tty
case "\${cmd##*/}" in
    ${_wrapped:-cmd}) mode=--pipes ;;
esac
case "\$cmd" in
    /*) ;;
    *) cmd=/system/bin/\$cmd ;;
esac
exec /usr/bin/env -i \${TERM:+TERM=\$TERM} /system/bin/sh /usr/local/lib/uburoot/android-run "\$mode" "\$cmd" "\$@"
EOF
if ! { chmod 755 "$WRAP_DIR/android.tmp" && mv -f "$WRAP_DIR/android.tmp" "$WRAP_DIR/android"; }; then
    ubu_warn "wrapper android failed"
fi

ubu_install_bridge || ubu_warn "cannot install the uburoot-* commands"

ubu_log "android env captured ($(grep -c '^export' "$ENV_FILE") vars), wrappers in /usr/local/bin"
exit 0

```

`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/boot.sh`:

```sh
#!/system/bin/sh
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Autostart at boot (Phase 5). Run by the root module's service.sh after
# sys.boot_completed, in the global mount namespace, in the background.
# Does nothing unless BOOT_START=1 in uburoot.conf (Settings → Start at boot).
#   1. waits until the shared storage is decrypted (first unlock), if /sdcard
#      is wanted inside Ubuntu;
#   2. mount.sh;
#   3. runs every executable /etc/uburoot-boot.d/NAME inside Ubuntu as root,
#      in name order (NAME: letters, digits, _ and - only, like run-parts, so
#      README, *.dpkg-old or *.disabled are skipped). Each one gets
#      BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT seconds; one still running after that is left
#      running and the next one starts (daemons should fork, e.g. sshd).
# Output goes to $UBU_BASE/boot.log (the previous boot's is boot.log.old).
# Usage: boot.sh [--now | --status]
#   --now     don't wait for the unlock, ignore BOOT_START (Settings → Run now)
#   --status  print module=enabled|disabled|removing|pending|missing and
#             boot_start=0|1 (the module dir is the same for Magisk, KernelSU
#             and APatch: /data/adb/modules/uburoot)
set -u
UBU_BIN_DIR=$(cd -P -- "$(dirname -- "$0")" && pwd -P)
# shellcheck source=common.sh
. "$UBU_BIN_DIR/common.sh"

ubu_require_root
ubu_init
ubu_load_config

NOW=0
STATUS=0
for _a in "$@"; do
    case "$_a" in
        --now) NOW=1 ;;
        --status) STATUS=1 ;;
        *) ubu_die "unknown option: $_a" ;;
    esac
done

BOOT_START=${BOOT_START:-0}
BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT=${BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT:-60}
case "$BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT" in
    '' | *[!0-9]*) BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT=60 ;;
esac
R=$ROOTFS
BOOT_D="$R/etc/uburoot-boot.d"

UBU_MODULES=${UBU_MODULES:-/data/adb}
if [ "$STATUS" = 1 ]; then
    _m="$UBU_MODULES/modules/uburoot"
    if [ -d "$UBU_MODULES/modules_update/uburoot" ]; then
        _s=pending # installed, active after the next reboot
    elif [ ! -d "$_m" ]; then
        _s=missing
    elif [ -e "$_m/remove" ]; then
        _s=removing
    elif [ -e "$_m/disable" ]; then
        _s=disabled
    else
        _s=enabled
    fi
    printf 'module=%s\nboot_start=%s\n' "$_s" "$BOOT_START"
    exit 0
fi

stamp() { date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S' 2>/dev/null; }
say() { printf '%s %s\n' "$(stamp)" "$*" >&2; }

if [ "$NOW" = 0 ] && [ "$BOOT_START" != 1 ]; then
    exit 0
fi
[ -x "$R/usr/bin/env" ] || ubu_die "Ubuntu is not installed ($R)"

if [ "$NOW" = 0 ] && [ "$MOUNT_SDCARD" = 1 ]; then
    # Up to 6 hours: a phone may sit at the lock screen after a reboot.
    _n=0
    while ! ubu_sdcard_source >/dev/null && [ $_n -lt 4320 ]; do
        [ $_n = 0 ] && say "waiting for the shared storage (unlock the phone)"
        sleep 5
        _n=$((_n + 1))
    done
fi

say "mounting"
"$UBU_SH" "$UBU_BIN_DIR/mount.sh" || ubu_die "mount.sh failed; boot scripts not run"

if [ ! -d "$BOOT_D" ]; then
    say "no $BOOT_D; nothing to start"
    exit 0
fi
_failed=0
for _f in "$BOOT_D"/*; do
    _name=${_f##*/}
    case "$_name" in
        '*' | *[!A-Za-z0-9_-]*) continue ;;
    esac
    if [ -L "$_f" ] || [ ! -f "$_f" ] || [ ! -x "$_f" ]; then
        continue
    fi
    say "starting $_name"
    # enter.sh: the same clean environment and groups as a root session.
    "$UBU_SH" "$UBU_BIN_DIR/enter.sh" root "/etc/uburoot-boot.d/$_name" </dev/null &
    _pid=$!
    _t=0
    while kill -0 "$_pid" 2>/dev/null && [ $_t -lt "$BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT" ]; do
        sleep 1
        _t=$((_t + 1))
    done
    if kill -0 "$_pid" 2>/dev/null; then
        say "$_name still running after ${BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT}s; left running, going on"
        continue
    fi
    if wait "$_pid"; then
        say "$_name: ok"
    else
        say "$_name: failed (exit $?)"
        _failed=1
    fi
done
say "boot scripts done"
exit $_failed

```

`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/bridge.sh`:

```sh
#!/system/bin/sh
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Android side of the app <-> Ubuntu bridge (Phase 4). Called by the app.
# Usage:
#   bridge.sh take ID DEST
#       Move a file that uburoot-open put in the rootfs outbox into DEST, a
#       file the app created in its own cache (so owner and SELinux label are
#       the app's). ID is the hex id from the broadcast.
#   bridge.sh import SRC NAME
#       Copy SRC (a file the app received through "Open in UbuRoot") into
#       ~/Shared/NAME of the default user, and print that directory (a path
#       inside Ubuntu). The copy runs INSIDE the chroot as that user, so a
#       symlink in the user's home can't make root write outside it.
#   bridge.sh cwd DIR
#       Exit 0 if DIR (a path inside Ubuntu, e.g. /sdcard/Download) is a
#       directory the default user can enter; mounts first, as a session would.
set -u
UBU_BIN_DIR=$(cd -P -- "$(dirname -- "$0")" && pwd -P)
# shellcheck source=common.sh
. "$UBU_BIN_DIR/common.sh"

ubu_require_root
ubu_init
ubu_load_config

R=$ROOTFS
OUTBOX="$R/var/lib/uburoot/outbox"

# Mounts as enter.sh does and sets the default user's uid/gid/home.
mount_and_set_user() {
    _user=${DEFAULT_USER:-root}
    case "$_user" in
        *[!a-z0-9_-]* | '') ubu_die "invalid user name: $_user" ;;
    esac
    "$UBU_SH" "$UBU_BIN_DIR/mount.sh" >/dev/null || ubu_die "mount.sh failed"
    _pw=$(bb awk -F: -v u="$_user" '$1 == u { print $3 ":" $4 ":" $6; exit }' "$R/etc/passwd")
    [ -n "$_pw" ] || ubu_die "user '$_user' does not exist in the rootfs"
    _uid=${_pw%%:*}
    _rest=${_pw#*:}
    _gid=${_rest%%:*}
    _home=${_rest#*:}
}

# as_user CMD...: run CMD inside the chroot as the default user, clean env.
as_user() {
    set -- /usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin "HOME=$_home" "$@"
    if [ "$_uid" != 0 ]; then
        set -- /usr/bin/setpriv --reuid="$_uid" --regid="$_gid" --init-groups -- "$@"
    fi
    bb chroot "$R" "$@"
}

case "${1:-}" in
    take)
        [ $# -eq 3 ] || ubu_die "usage: bridge.sh take ID DEST"
        _id=$2
        _dest=$3
        case "$_id" in
            '' | *[!0-9a-f]*) ubu_die "bad id: $_id" ;;
        esac
        [ "${#_id}" -eq 16 ] || ubu_die "bad id: $_id"
        _src="$OUTBOX/$_id"
        # The outbox is root-only (0700), written by uburoot-send as root.
        if [ -L "$_src" ] || [ ! -f "$_src" ]; then
            ubu_die "no such outbox file: $_id"
        fi
        if [ -L "$_dest" ] || [ ! -f "$_dest" ]; then
            ubu_die "destination must be an existing regular file: $_dest"
        fi
        cat "$_src" >"$_dest" || ubu_die "copy failed"
        rm -f "$_src"
        ;;
    import)
        [ $# -eq 3 ] || ubu_die "usage: bridge.sh import SRC NAME"
        _src=$2
        _name=${3##*/}
        case "$_name" in
            '' | . | ..) _name=shared-file ;;
        esac
        [ -f "$_src" ] || ubu_die "no such file: $_src"
        mount_and_set_user
        # Inside the chroot, as the user: mkdir ~/Shared, pick a free name, write
        # stdin there and print the directory. The name travels as an argument.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2016 # expanded by the shell inside the chroot
        _script='set -e
d="$HOME/Shared"
mkdir -p "$d"
n=$1
i=1
while [ -e "$d/$n" ] || [ -L "$d/$n" ]; do
    n="$i-$1"
    i=$((i + 1))
done
cat >"$d/$n"
printf "%s\n" "$d"'
        as_user /bin/sh -c "$_script" sh "$_name" <"$_src" || ubu_die "import failed"
        ;;
    cwd)
        [ $# -eq 2 ] || ubu_die "usage: bridge.sh cwd DIR"
        case "$2" in
            /*) ;;
            *) ubu_die "not an absolute path: $2" ;;
        esac
        mount_and_set_user
        as_user /bin/sh -c '[ -d "$1" ] && [ -x "$1" ]' sh "$2" </dev/null
        exit $?
        ;;
    *) ubu_die "usage: bridge.sh take ID DEST | import SRC NAME | cwd DIR" ;;
esac
exit 0

```

`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/changes.sh`:

```sh
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Sourced by kernel.sh (uburoot-kernel) and hardware.sh (uburoot-hw): the change log
# that records every change UbuRoot makes to the phone's kernel or hardware state,
# so that Stop all ("kernel.sh -y revert") and "Revert changes" can undo it.
# Not executable on its own. Inside Ubuntu it lives in /usr/local/lib/uburoot/.
#
# The caller sets: MODE (android|ubuntu), STATE (the state directory) and the
# functions die and say. Test-only overrides: UBU_BOOT_ID_FILE, UBU_SYSCTL_DIR,
# UBU_SELINUX_ENFORCE, UBU_PROC_MODULES, UBU_RMMOD, UBU_POWER_DIR, UBU_ACMD.
#
# One line per change, "TYPE NAME ORIGINAL":
#   sysctl NAME VALUE        a /proc/sys setting (kernel.kptr_restrict)
#   selinux - 0|1            the SELinux mode
#   module NAME -            a module loaded through UbuRoot
#   file PATH VALUE          a sysfs file (CPU governor, LED brightness...)
#   node PATH UID:GID:MODE   owner and mode of a device node
#   wakelock NAME -          a kernel wake lock held through UbuRoot
#   takeover CLASS STATE     a device taken from Android (e.g. wifi 1:1)
# Only the first change of a setting is recorded: that holds the true original.
# Nothing persists across reboots: a log from an earlier boot is discarded.

CHANGES="$STATE/changes"
LOCK="$STATE/.lock"
BOOTID_FILE=${UBU_BOOT_ID_FILE:-/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id}
SYSCTL=${UBU_SYSCTL_DIR:-/proc/sys}
ENFORCE=${UBU_SELINUX_ENFORCE:-/sys/fs/selinux/enforce}
PROCMOD=${UBU_PROC_MODULES:-/proc/modules}
RMMOD=${UBU_RMMOD:-rmmod}
POWER=${UBU_POWER_DIR:-/sys/power}

unlock() {
    rm -f "$LOCK/pid" 2>/dev/null
    rmdir "$LOCK" 2>/dev/null
}
# lock: take the change log (wait up to 20 s); lock_try: return 1 if it is taken.
lock() { _lock wait; }
lock_try() { _lock try; }
_lock() {
    mkdir -p "$STATE" 2>/dev/null
    [ "$MODE" = android ] && chmod 700 "$STATE" 2>/dev/null
    _i=0
    until mkdir "$LOCK" 2>/dev/null; do
        # A holder killed by a signal its traps don't cover leaves the lock behind:
        # break it when the recorded owner is gone (Android and Ubuntu share pids).
        _o=$(cat "$LOCK/pid" 2>/dev/null)
        if [ -n "$_o" ] && ! kill -0 "$_o" 2>/dev/null; then
            unlock
            continue
        fi
        [ "$1" = try ] && return 1
        _i=$((_i + 1))
        [ "$_i" -lt 20 ] || die "change log is locked ($LOCK)"
        sleep 1
    done
    printf '%s\n' "$$" >"$LOCK/pid"
    trap unlock EXIT
    # PIPE: "uburoot-kernel status | head" must not leave the lock (dash runs no
    # EXIT trap when a signal kills it).
    trap 'exit 1' INT TERM HUP PIPE
    # A log from an earlier boot describes changes the reboot already undid.
    _now=$(cat "$BOOTID_FILE" 2>/dev/null)
    if [ "$(cat "$STATE/boot_id" 2>/dev/null)" != "$_now" ]; then
        rm -f "$CHANGES"
        printf '%s\n' "$_now" >"$STATE/boot_id"
    fi
    [ -f "$CHANGES" ] || : >"$CHANGES"
}
rec_get() { # TYPE NAME -> recorded original (exit 1 if none)
    bb awk -v t="$1" -v n="$2" '$1 == t && $2 == n { print substr($0, length($1 $2) + 3); f = 1; exit } END { exit !f }' "$CHANGES" 2>/dev/null
}
rec_add() { # TYPE NAME VALUE (only the first time)
    rec_get "$1" "$2" >/dev/null && return 0
    printf '%s %s %s\n' "$1" "$2" "$3" >>"$CHANGES"
}
rec_del() { # TYPE NAME
    bb awk -v t="$1" -v n="$2" '!($1 == t && $2 == n)' "$CHANGES" >"$CHANGES.tmp" && mv -f "$CHANGES.tmp" "$CHANGES"
}
rec_count() { bb awk 'NF' "$CHANGES" 2>/dev/null | bb wc -l | tr -d ' '; }

# A value into a sysfs or /proc file in ONE write(): sysfs stores each write() as a
# separate value. mksh has no builtin printf, and toybox's printf writes "2803200"
# and "\n" separately (strace on the S9), so the "\n" alone fails with EINVAL.
# The shell's builtin echo (mksh, dash) writes the line at once.
wr() { # VALUE FILE
    echo "$1" >"$2"
}

loaded() { bb awk '{ print $1 }' "$PROCMOD" 2>/dev/null; }

# An Android tool (cmd, settings, svc...): directly on the Android side, through
# the bionic wrappers inside Ubuntu.
acmd() {
    if [ -n "${UBU_ACMD:-}" ]; then
        "$UBU_ACMD" "$@"
    elif [ "$MODE" = android ]; then
        "$@"
    else
        /usr/local/bin/android "$@"
    fi
}

# Processes started from Ubuntu's own binaries (never Android's /vendor ones of the
# same name): their executable ends in /usr/sbin/NAME (or /usr/bin/NAME).
kill_ubuntu_exe() { # NAME...
    # One ls + awk, not a readlink per process: from the app's cgroup a fork per
    # process took ~20 s for a Wi-Fi release on the S9 (see ubu_chroot_pids).
    # shellcheck disable=SC2012 # symlink targets of /proc/*/exe: no odd file names here
    _pids=$(ls -l /proc/[0-9]*/exe 2>/dev/null | bb awk -v names="$*" '
        BEGIN { n = split(names, w, " ") }
        $(NF - 1) == "->" {
            for (i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
                if ($NF ~ ("/usr/s?bin/" w[i] "$")) {
                    split($(NF - 2), a, "/")
                    print a[3]
                }
            }
        }')
    # shellcheck disable=SC2086 # a list of pids
    [ -z "$_pids" ] || kill $_pids 2>/dev/null
    return 0
}

# Wi-Fi given back to Android. STATE is "WIFI:SCAN" as it was before the takeover.
wifi_release() { # STATE
    kill_ubuntu_exe wpa_supplicant hostapd dhclient dhcpcd iwd
    _w=${1%%:*}
    _s=${1#*:}
    _ok=0
    if [ "$_s" = 1 ]; then acmd cmd wifi set-scan-always-available enabled >/dev/null 2>&1 || _ok=1; fi
    if [ "$_w" = 1 ]; then acmd cmd wifi set-wifi-enabled enabled >/dev/null 2>&1 || _ok=1; fi
    return "$_ok"
}

revert_one() { # TYPE NAME VALUE -> 0 if undone
    case "$1" in
        module)
            if loaded | bb grep -qx "$2"; then
                "$RMMOD" "$2" 2>/dev/null || { say "could not unload $2 (in use?)" >&2; return 1; }
            fi
            ;;
        sysctl)
            _f="$SYSCTL/$(printf '%s' "$2" | tr '.' '/')"
            if wr "$3" "$_f" 2>/dev/null; then
                :
            elif [ "$2" = kernel.kptr_restrict ] && [ "$3" -gt 2 ] 2>/dev/null &&
                wr 2 "$_f" 2>/dev/null; then
                # Some kernels boot with a value above 2 (the S9: 4) that a write
                # can't set again (0-2 only); the kernel treats >= 2 alike: hidden.
                say "kernel.kptr_restrict: $3 can't be written back; set 2 (same effect)"
            else
                say "could not restore $2 = $3" >&2
                return 1
            fi
            ;;
        selinux)
            wr "$3" "$ENFORCE" 2>/dev/null || { say "could not restore SELinux" >&2; return 1; }
            ;;
        file)
            # Gone (an unplugged device, an offline CPU's files) = nothing to restore.
            [ -e "$2" ] || return 0
            wr "$3" "$2" 2>/dev/null || { say "could not restore $2 = $3" >&2; return 1; }
            ;;
        node)
            [ -e "$2" ] || return 0
            if ! { chown "${3%:*}" "$2" 2>/dev/null && chmod "${3##*:}" "$2" 2>/dev/null; }; then
                say "could not restore $2 ($3)" >&2
                return 1
            fi
            ;;
        wakelock)
            wr "$2" "$POWER/wake_unlock" 2>/dev/null
            ;;
        takeover)
            case "$2" in
                wifi) wifi_release "$3" || { say "could not give Wi-Fi back to Android" >&2; return 1; } ;;
                *) say "unknown takeover $2" >&2 ;;
            esac
            ;;
        *) ;;
    esac
    return 0
}

# Undo every recorded change, newest first. Changes that can't be undone stay.
revert() {
    _left=""
    _done=0
    _lines=$(bb awk '{ l[NR] = $0 } END { for (i = NR; i > 0; i--) print l[i] }' "$CHANGES")
    _ifs=$IFS
    IFS='
'
    for _l in $_lines; do
        IFS=$_ifs
        _t=${_l%% *}
        _r=${_l#* }
        _n=${_r%% *}
        _v=${_r#* }
        if revert_one "$_t" "$_n" "$_v"; then
            _done=$((_done + 1))
            say "reverted: $_t $_n"
        else
            _left="$_l
$_left"
        fi
    done
    IFS=$_ifs
    printf '%s' "$_left" | bb awk 'NF' >"$CHANGES"
    if [ -s "$CHANGES" ]; then
        say "$(rec_count) change(s) could not be reverted" >&2
        return 1
    fi
    [ "$_done" -gt 0 ] || say "nothing to revert"
    return 0
}

# What mount.sh granted, from /run/uburoot/access inside Ubuntu (KEY=0|1 lines).
ubuntu_access() { # KEY -> 0|1
    bb awk -F= -v k="$1" '$1 == k { v = $2 } END { print (v == "1" ? 1 : 0) }' "${UBU_ACCESS_FILE:-/run/uburoot/access}" 2>/dev/null || echo 0
}

```

`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/chroot-cmd.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Installed by android-env into the rootfs as /usr/local/lib/uburoot/uburoot-cmd;
# /usr/local/bin/uburoot-{notify,toast,vibrate,clip,open} and xdg-open link to it.
# Runs inside Ubuntu as any user and hands over to the root helper
# (uburoot-send) through the sudo rule in /etc/sudoers.d/80-uburoot.
SEND=/usr/local/lib/uburoot/uburoot-send

name=${0##*/}
case "$name" in
    xdg-open) cmd=open ;;
    uburoot-*) cmd=${name#uburoot-} ;;
    *)
        cmd=${1:-}
        [ $# -gt 0 ] && shift
        ;;
esac

case "${1:-}" in
    -h | --help)
        case "$cmd" in
            notify) echo "usage: uburoot-notify TITLE [TEXT]    Android notification" ;;
            toast) echo "usage: uburoot-toast TEXT             short message on screen" ;;
            vibrate) echo "usage: uburoot-vibrate [MS]           vibrate (default 300 ms)" ;;
            clip) echo "usage: uburoot-clip [TEXT]            copy TEXT (or stdin) to Android's clipboard" ;;
            open) echo "usage: uburoot-open FILE|URL          open with an Android app (also: xdg-open)" ;;
            *) echo "usage: uburoot-notify|toast|vibrate|clip|open ..." ;;
        esac
        exit 0
        ;;
esac

if [ "$(id -u)" = 0 ]; then
    exec "$SEND" "$cmd" "$@"
fi
if ! command -v sudo >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "uburoot: sudo is not installed (apt install sudo), or run this as root" >&2
    exit 1
fi
exec sudo -n "$SEND" "$cmd" "$@"

```

`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/chroot-send.sh`:

```sh
#!/bin/sh
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Installed by android-env into the rootfs as /usr/local/lib/uburoot/uburoot-send.
# Runs INSIDE the chroot, as root: the uburoot-* commands call it through a
# sudo rule that allows exactly this file. It sends one command to the UbuRoot
# app with `am broadcast` (only root, system or the app itself may: the
# receiver is protected by a signature permission, and a chroot user's uid
# holds no Android permission at all).
#
# Usage: uburoot-send notify TITLE [TEXT] | toast TEXT | vibrate [MS]
#                     | clip [TEXT] (stdin without TEXT) | open FILE|URL
#
# Files are read with the CALLER's identity (setpriv), never as root, so a user
# can't open what they can't read. A file is copied into a root-only outbox
# (/var/lib/uburoot/outbox) and the app takes it from there (bridge.sh take).
set -eu
PATH=/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin
export PATH
umask 077

APP=com.reinanbr.uburoot
OUTBOX=/var/lib/uburoot/outbox
MAX_TEXT=100000 # bytes; a broadcast travels in one binder transaction (1 MB)

die() {
    printf 'uburoot: %s\n' "$*" >&2
    exit 1
}

[ "$(id -u)" = 0 ] || die "uburoot-send must run as root (the uburoot-* commands call it through sudo)"
[ $# -ge 1 ] || die "usage: uburoot-send notify|toast|vibrate|clip|open ARGS..."
cmd=$1
shift

# Who asked: sudo sets SUDO_UID/SUDO_GID; unset when root calls it directly.
uid=${SUDO_UID:-0}
gid=${SUDO_GID:-0}
case "$uid$gid" in
    *[!0-9]*) die "bad SUDO_UID/SUDO_GID" ;;
esac

as_caller() {
    if [ "$uid" = 0 ]; then
        "$@"
    else
        setpriv --reuid="$uid" --regid="$gid" --init-groups -- "$@"
    fi
}

check_text() {
    [ "$(printf '%s' "$1" | wc -c)" -le "$MAX_TEXT" ] || die "text longer than $MAX_TEXT bytes"
}

# send EXTRA_ARGS... : broadcast to the app's receiver and wait for its answer.
send() {
    _opts="--user 0 --receiver-foreground"
    if [ "$cmd" = open ]; then
        # Android 10+ blocks activity starts from the background; this option
        # (root may use it) lets the receiver open the viewer anyway.
        _sdk=$(getprop ro.build.version.sdk 2>/dev/null) || _sdk=0
        case "$_sdk" in
            '' | *[!0-9]*) _sdk=0 ;;
        esac
        if [ "$_sdk" -ge 29 ]; then _opts="$_opts --allow-background-activity-starts"; fi
    fi
    [ -x /usr/local/bin/am ] || die "Android integration is off (no am in Ubuntu); turn it on in the app's settings"
    # shellcheck disable=SC2086 # _opts is a list of fixed options
    _out=$(am broadcast $_opts -a "$APP.CMD" -n "$APP/.bridge.CommandReceiver" --es cmd "$cmd" "$@" 2>&1) ||
        die "am broadcast failed: $_out"
    # The receiver answers RESULT_OK (-1), or an error message as result data.
    case "$_out" in
        *"result=-1"*) return 0 ;;
        *'data="'*)
            _msg=${_out#*data=\"}
            die "${_msg%\"*}"
            ;;
        *) die "the UbuRoot app did not answer (is it installed?)" ;;
    esac
}

case "$cmd" in
    notify)
        if [ $# -lt 1 ] || [ $# -gt 2 ]; then die "usage: uburoot-notify TITLE [TEXT]"; fi
        check_text "$1${2:-}"
        send --es title "$1" --es text "${2:-}"
        ;;
    toast)
        [ $# -eq 1 ] || die "usage: uburoot-toast TEXT"
        check_text "$1"
        send --es text "$1"
        ;;
    vibrate)
        [ $# -le 1 ] || die "usage: uburoot-vibrate [MS]   (default 300, at most 10000)"
        _ms=${1:-300}
        case "$_ms" in
            '' | *[!0-9]*) die "usage: uburoot-vibrate [MS]   (milliseconds)" ;;
        esac
        if [ "$_ms" -lt 1 ] || [ "$_ms" -gt 10000 ]; then die "vibrate: 1..10000 ms"; fi
        send --es ms "$_ms"
        ;;
    clip)
        [ $# -le 1 ] || die "usage: uburoot-clip [TEXT]   (reads stdin without TEXT)"
        if [ $# -eq 1 ]; then
            _text=$1
        else
            # $(...) drops trailing newlines, as copying a command's output should.
            _text=$(head -c $((MAX_TEXT + 1)))
        fi
        check_text "$_text"
        send --es text "$_text"
        ;;
    open)
        if [ $# -ne 1 ] || [ -z "$1" ]; then die "usage: uburoot-open FILE|URL"; fi
        case "$1" in
            [a-zA-Z]*://* | mailto:* | tel:* | geo:* | sms:*)
                send --es url "$1"
                exit 0
                ;;
            file://*) _path=${1#file://} ;;
            *) _path=$1 ;;
        esac
        as_caller test -f "$_path" || die "open: not a readable file: $_path"
        as_caller test -r "$_path" || die "open: permission denied: $_path"
        mkdir -p "$OUTBOX"
        chmod 700 /var/lib/uburoot "$OUTBOX"
        # Leftovers the app never took (app gone, phone rebooted...).
        find "$OUTBOX" -type f -mmin +1440 -delete 2>/dev/null || true
        _id=$(od -An -tx1 -N8 /dev/urandom | tr -d ' \n')
        # Removed again on any failure; on success the app takes it (bridge.sh take).
        trap 'rm -f "$OUTBOX/$_id"' EXIT
        # Read as the caller, written by root into the root-only outbox.
        as_caller cat -- "$_path" >"$OUTBOX/$_id" || die "open: cannot read $_path"
        _mime=""
        if command -v file >/dev/null 2>&1; then
            _mime=$(file -b --mime-type "$OUTBOX/$_id" 2>/dev/null) || _mime=""
        fi
        send --es id "$_id" --es name "${_path##*/}" --es mime "$_mime"
        trap - EXIT
        ;;
    *) die "unknown command: $cmd (notify, toast, vibrate, clip, open)" ;;
esac

```

`app/src/main/assets/uburoot/bin/common.sh`:

```sh
# shellcheck shell=sh
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Variables defined here are consumed by the scripts that source this file.
# shellcheck disable=SC2034
# UbuRoot common definitions. Sourced by every script; never executed directly.
# POSIX sh only: these run under Android's mksh (/system/bin/sh).

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Paths (the env overrides exist for tests only; the app never sets them)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
UBU_BASE="${UBU_BASE:-/data/local/uburoot}"
UBU_BIN="${UBU_BIN:-$UBU_BASE/bin}"
ROOTFS="${ROOTFS:-$UBU_BASE/rootfs}"
UBU_CONFIG="${UBU_CONFIG:-$UBU_BASE/uburoot.conf}"
UBU_MOUNTS_FILE="${UBU_MOUNTS_FILE:-/proc/self/mounts}"
UBU_MOUNTINFO_FILE="${UBU_MOUNTINFO_FILE:-/proc/self/mountinfo}"
UBU_INOTIFY_FILE="${UBU_INOTIFY_FILE:-/proc/sys/fs/inotify/max_user_watches}"
# Kernel changes made through uburoot-kernel, reverted by Stop all (kernel.sh).
UBU_STATE="${UBU_STATE:-$UBU_BASE/state}"

# Directories inside the rootfs that only ever serve as bind-mount targets.
# They must be empty (rmdir-able) before the rootfs may be deleted.
UBU_BIND_TARGETS="data sdcard system vendor product system_ext odm apex linkerconfig"
# Android partitions bound recursively at the same path inside the rootfs.
UBU_ANDROID_PARTS="/system /vendor /product /system_ext /odm /apex /linkerconfig"
# Kernel filesystems for KERNEL_ACCESS=1 (mount.sh), as TYPE:PATH:HOW. The host's
# mount of TYPE is bound at PATH inside the rootfs (so Ubuntu sees the same
# objects: netd's pinned BPF maps, the USB gadget, Android's cgroups); where the
# host has none, HOW=new mounts a fresh instance and HOW=bind skips it. PATH
# must already exist in the chroot's own sysfs/proc (the kernel creates those
# directories only when it supports the filesystem).
UBU_KERNEL_FS="configfs:/sys/kernel/config:new debugfs:/sys/kernel/debug:new
tracefs:/sys/kernel/tracing:new bpf:/sys/fs/bpf:new cgroup2:/sys/fs/cgroup:bind
pstore:/sys/fs/pstore:new selinuxfs:/sys/fs/selinux:bind securityfs:/sys/kernel/security:new
fusectl:/sys/fs/fuse/connections:new binfmt_misc:/proc/sys/fs/binfmt_misc:new"
# Where ROMs keep kernel modules (bound read-only for modprobe, KERNEL_ACCESS=1).
UBU_MODULE_SOURCES="/vendor_dlkm/lib/modules /vendor/lib/modules /system_dlkm/lib/modules
/odm/lib/modules /lib/modules"

# Version of the integration files setup.sh writes into the rootfs. Bump it
# together with a new migrate_N in setup.sh; the app runs `setup.sh --migrate`
# when /etc/uburoot-release has an older SCHEMA.
UBU_SCHEMA=1
UBU_PORTS_MIRROR_DEFAULT=http://ports.ubuntu.com/ubuntu-ports/

if [ -x /system/bin/sh ]; then UBU_SH=/system/bin/sh; else UBU_SH="sh"; fi

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Logging
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
ubu_log() { printf '[uburoot] %s\n' "$*" >&2; }
ubu_warn() { printf '[uburoot] WARNING: %s\n' "$*" >&2; }
ubu_die() { printf '[uburoot] ERROR: %s\n' "$*" >&2; exit 1; }

ubu_require_root() {
    [ "${UBU_ALLOW_NONROOT:-0}" = 1 ] && return 0
    [ "$(id -u)" = 0 ] || ubu_die "must run as root (use: su --mount-master -c ...)"
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Config: defaults, then the app-written file (root-owned shell assignments)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
ubu_load_config() {
    MOUNT_SDCARD=1
    ANDROID_INTEGRATION=1
    DNS_SERVERS="1.1.1.1 9.9.9.9"
    DEFAULT_USER=""
    INIT_COMMAND=""
    UBU_HOSTNAME="uburoot"
    SDCARD_SOURCE=""
    INOTIFY_MAX_WATCHES=524288
    BOOT_START=0
    BOOT_SCRIPT_TIMEOUT=60
    APT_MIRROR=""
    KERNEL_ACCESS=0
    HARDWARE_ACCESS=0
    if [ -f "$UBU_CONFIG" ]; then
        # shellcheck source=/dev/null
        . "$UBU_CONFIG"
    fi
}

# Where /sdcard comes from: SDCARD_SOURCE, else the first candidate that looks
# real. Before the user's first unlock (file-based encryption) the names in
# /data/media/0 are encrypted, so none matches yet (boot.sh waits for it).
ubu_sdcard_source() {
    if [ -n "$SDCARD_SOURCE" ]; then
        printf '%s' "$SDCARD_SOURCE"
        return 0
    fi
    # /data/media/0 first: the FUSE view (/storage/emulated/0) returns EFAULT
    # to every non-root uid that isn't an app (MediaProvider can't map it to a
    # package), so the chroot user couldn't use it. Group aid_media_rw (1023)
    # gives the user access; the cost is no MediaStore notification.
    for _s in /data/media/0 /storage/emulated/0 /mnt/user/0/emulated/0; do
        if [ -d "$_s/Android" ] || [ -d "$_s/Download" ]; then
            printf '%s' "$_s"
            return 0
        fi
    done
    return 1
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Busybox / toybox
#   Magisk, KernelSU and APatch each ship a full busybox; fall back to toybox
#   (Android's /system/bin applets) when none is found.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
BB=""
BB_KIND="none"
UBU_TOYBOX_APPLETS=""

ubu_detect_busybox() {
    if [ -n "${UBU_BB:-}" ]; then
        if [ "$UBU_BB" = none ]; then BB=""; BB_KIND=none; else BB="$UBU_BB"; BB_KIND=busybox; fi
        return 0
    fi
    for _c in /data/adb/magisk/busybox /data/adb/ksu/bin/busybox /data/adb/ap/bin/busybox; do
        if [ -x "$_c" ]; then
            BB="$_c"
            BB_KIND=busybox
            return 0
        fi
    done
    if command -v toybox >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        BB=toybox
        BB_KIND=toybox
        UBU_TOYBOX_APPLETS=" $(toybox 2>/dev/null | tr '\n' ' ') "
    fi
}

# bb APPLET ARGS... : run an applet through the detected busybox/toybox.
bb() {
    case "$BB_KIND" in
        busybox) "$BB" "$@" ;;
        toybox)
            case "$UBU_TOYBOX_APPLETS" in
                *" $1 "*) toybox "$@" ;;
                *) "$@" ;;
            esac
            ;;
        *) "$@" ;;
    esac
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Root implementation (Magisk / KernelSU / APatch). All three accept
# -M/--mount-master (verified in their su sources; see CLAUDE.md).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
ubu_detect_root_impl() {
    UBU_ROOT_IMPL=unknown
    UBU_ROOT_VERSION=""
    _v=$(su -v 2>/dev/null) || _v=""
    case "$_v" in
        *:MAGISKSU*) UBU_ROOT_IMPL=magisk ;;
        *:KernelSU*) UBU_ROOT_IMPL=kernelsu ;;
        *:APatch*) UBU_ROOT_IMPL=apatch ;;
    esac
    if [ "$UBU_ROOT_IMPL" != unknown ]; then
        UBU_ROOT_VERSION=${_v%%:*}
        return 0
    fi
    if [ -d /data/adb/ksu ]; then
        UBU_ROOT_IMPL=kernelsu
    elif [ -d /data/adb/ap ]; then
        UBU_ROOT_IMPL=apatch
    elif [ -d /data/adb/magisk ]; then
        UBU_ROOT_IMPL=magisk
    fi
}
UBU_MOUNT_MASTER_FLAG="--mount-master"

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Path validation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Canonicalize an existing directory (resolves symlinks). Prints nothing on failure.
ubu_canon_dir() { (cd -P -- "$1" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P); }

# Refuse paths that could ever point at real device data.
ubu_validate_paths() {
    _ws=$(printf ' \t\n_'); _ws=${_ws%_}
    case "$UBU_BASE$ROOTFS" in
        *[$_ws]*) ubu_die "paths must not contain whitespace" ;;
    esac
    case "$UBU_BASE" in
        /*/*) ;;
        *) ubu_die "UBU_BASE must be an absolute path at least two levels deep: '$UBU_BASE'" ;;
    esac
    case "$ROOTFS" in
        "$UBU_BASE"/?*) ;;
        *) ubu_die "ROOTFS ($ROOTFS) must be inside UBU_BASE ($UBU_BASE)" ;;
    esac
    case "/$UBU_BASE/$ROOTFS/" in
        */../* | */./*) ubu_die "paths must not contain '.' or '..' components" ;;
    esac
    # Resolve symlinks so that comparisons against /proc/self/mounts are exact.
    if [ -d "$UBU_BASE" ]; then
        _c=$(ubu_canon_dir "$UBU_BASE") && [ -n "$_c" ] && UBU_BASE=$_c
    fi
    if [ -d "$ROOTFS" ]; then
        _c=$(ubu_canon_dir "$ROOTFS") && [ -n "$_c" ] && ROOTFS=$_c
        case "$ROOTFS" in
            "$UBU_BASE"/?*) ;;
            *) ubu_die "ROOTFS resolves outside UBU_BASE: $ROOTFS" ;;
        esac
    fi
}

ubu_rootfs_installed() { [ -x "$ROOTFS/usr/bin/env" ] && [ -x "$ROOTFS/bin/bash" ]; }

# ubu_elf_arch FILE: Debian architecture of an ELF binary (arm64, armhf, amd64,
# i386), from e_machine. FILE must not be a symlink: an absolute link inside a
# rootfs would resolve on the host.
ubu_elf_arch() {
    if [ ! -f "$1" ] || [ -L "$1" ]; then return 1; fi
    case "$(bb od -An -tx1 -j18 -N2 "$1" 2>/dev/null | tr -d ' \n')" in
        b700) printf arm64 ;;
        2800) printf armhf ;;
        3e00) printf amd64 ;;
        0300) printf i386 ;;
        *) return 1 ;;
    esac
}

# Debian architecture this CPU runs natively (uname -m).
ubu_native_arch() {
    case "$(uname -m)" in
        aarch64 | arm64) printf arm64 ;;
        armv7* | armv8l) printf armhf ;;
        x86_64) printf amd64 ;;
        i?86) printf i386 ;;
        *) return 1 ;;
    esac
}

# The rootfs's integration schema (/etc/uburoot-release), 0 when absent.
ubu_rootfs_schema() {
    _s=$(bb awk -F= '$1 == "SCHEMA" { print $2; exit }' "$ROOTFS/etc/uburoot-release" 2>/dev/null)
    case "$_s" in '' | *[!0-9]*) printf 0 ;; *) printf '%s' "$_s" ;; esac
}

# ubu_valid_mirror URL: an http:// Ubuntu mirror. Plain http on purpose: Ubuntu
# Base has no CA certificates, so apt can't use https before the first install
# (apt checks every index against the archive key either way).
ubu_valid_mirror() {
    case "$1" in
        http://?*/) ;;
        *) return 1 ;;
    esac
    case "${1#http://}" in
        *[!A-Za-z0-9._~:/-]* | /* | *//*) return 1 ;;
    esac
    return 0
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Root quality: UbuRoot requires a root with full kernel access. A root
# manager can hand out less: KernelSU's App Profile sets CapEff/CapPrm/CapBnd
# from its capability field and can switch the SELinux domain
# (kernel/policy/app_profile.c, escape_with_root_profile). Seccomp and
# NoNewPrivs would also cut kernel access.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Hex mask of every capability up to cap_last_cap N, without leading zeros.
ubu_cap_full_mask() {
    bb awk -v n="$1" 'BEGIN {
        b = n + 1; s = ""
        for (i = 0; i < int(b / 4); i++) s = s "f"
        if (b % 4) s = substr("137", b % 4, 1) s
        print s
    }'
}

# key=value lines about this shell's root; full_root=yes|no is the verdict.
ubu_root_report() {
    _last=$(cat /proc/sys/kernel/cap_last_cap 2>/dev/null)
    case "$_last" in '' | *[!0-9]*) _last="" ;; esac
    _mask=$(ubu_cap_full_mask "${_last:-0}")
    bb awk -v full="$_mask" -v last="$_last" '
        /^CapEff:/ { e = $2 }
        /^CapBnd:/ { b = $2 }
        /^NoNewPrivs:/ { n = $2 }
        /^Seccomp:/ { s = $2 }
        END {
            ee = e; sub(/^0+/, "", ee)
            bn = b; sub(/^0+/, "", bn)
            # NoNewPrivs is only listed since Linux 4.10: absent means not set.
            ok = (last != "" && ee == full && bn == full && (n == "" || n == 0) && s == 0)
            print "cap_last_cap=" last
            print "cap_eff=" e
            print "cap_bnd=" b
            print "no_new_privs=" (n == "" ? 0 : n)
            print "seccomp=" s
            print "full_root=" (ok ? "yes" : "no")
        }' "/proc/$$/status"
    printf 'selinux=%s\n' "$(tr -d '\000' </proc/self/attr/current 2>/dev/null)"
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Kernel interfaces for the chroot (mount.sh)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# ubu_fs_mount TYPE PREFERRED: a host mountpoint of filesystem TYPE outside the
# rootfs, PREFERRED when it is one. Android 12 mounts configfs at /config.
ubu_fs_mount() {
    bb awk -v t="$1" -v p="$2" -v r="$ROOTFS" '
        $3 != t || $2 == r || index($2, r "/") == 1 { next }
        $2 == p { print p; found = 1; exit }
        first == "" { first = $2 }
        END { if (!found && first != "") print first }' "$UBU_MOUNTS_FILE"
}

# The ROM's kernel modules for the running kernel: the first candidate with a
# modules.dep (where modprobe looks).
ubu_module_dir() {
    _kv=$(uname -r)
    for _d in "/lib/modules/$_kv" /vendor_dlkm/lib/modules /vendor/lib/modules \
        "/system_dlkm/lib/modules/$_kv" /system_dlkm/lib/modules /odm/lib/modules; do
        if [ -f "$_d/modules.dep" ]; then
            printf '%s' "$_d"
            return 0
        fi
    done
    return 1
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Mount table helpers (paths in /proc/self/mounts escape ' ' as \040 etc.;
# our paths are validated whitespace-free, so raw comparison is exact)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
ubu_unescape() {
    case "$1" in
        *\\*) printf '%b' "$(printf '%s' "$1" | sed 's/\\\([0-7][0-7][0-7]\)/\\0\1/g')" ;;
        *) printf '%s' "$1" ;;
    esac
}

# One awk instead of a shell read loop: with Magisk's Zygisk the mount table
# has ~2000 lines (magic mount binds every file of /system/lib*).
ubu_is_mounted() {
    bb awk -v p="$1" '$2 == p { f = 1; exit } END { exit !f }' "$UBU_MOUNTS_FILE"
}

# Mountpoints equal to or below $1, deepest first (stacked mounts repeat).
ubu_mounts_under() {
    bb awk -v p="$1" '$2 == p || index($2, p "/") == 1 { n = gsub("/", "/", $2); print n " " $2 }' \
        "$UBU_MOUNTS_FILE" | bb sort -rn | bb cut -d' ' -f2-
}

ubu_count_mounts_under() { ubu_mounts_under "$1" | bb wc -l | tr -d ' '; }

# Exit 0 if any mount at/below $1 is still in a shared peer group.
ubu_any_shared_under() {
    [ -r "$UBU_MOUNTINFO_FILE" ] || return 1
    bb awk -v p="$1" '
        $5 == p || index($5, p "/") == 1 {
            for (i = 7; i <= NF && $i != "-"; i++) if ($i ~ /^shared:/) found = 1
        }
        END { exit found ? 0 : 1 }' "$UBU_MOUNTINFO_FILE"
}

# ubu_set_propagation private|rprivate|slave|rslave TARGET
# Verified through mountinfo: no mount at/below TARGET may remain shared.
ubu_set_propagation() {
    bb mount --make-"$1" "$2" 2>/dev/null || bb mount -o "$1" "$2" 2>/dev/null || return 1
    if ubu_any_shared_under "$2"; then return 1; fi
    return 0
}

# ubu_mount_fs TYPE SOURCE TARGET [OPTIONS]  (idempotent)
ubu_mount_fs() {
    if ubu_is_mounted "$3"; then return 0; fi
    mkdir -p "$3" || return 1
    if [ -n "${4:-}" ]; then
        bb mount -t "$1" -o "$4" "$2" "$3"
    else
        bb mount -t "$1" "$2" "$3"
    fi
}

# ubu_bind bind|rbind SOURCE TARGET  (idempotent; result is always rslave)
# If the propagation change fails we must NOT unmount: on an rbind tree that
# is still shared, the umount itself would propagate to the real mounts.
ubu_bind() {
    if ubu_is_mounted "$3"; then return 0; fi
    if [ -d "$2" ]; then
        mkdir -p "$3" || return 1
    else
        mkdir -p "${3%/*}" && { [ -e "$3" ] || : >"$3"; } || return 1
    fi
    bb mount -o "$1" "$2" "$3" || return 1
    ubu_set_propagation rslave "$3" ||
        ubu_die "bound $2 at $3 but could not make it rslave; NOT unmounting (unsafe). Reboot to clear it."
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CRITICAL SAFETY RULE
#   Never rm -rf anything under UBU_BASE while mounts exist below it: with
#   /data and /sdcard bind-mounted inside, that would erase the phone's real
#   data. This is the ONLY function allowed to delete under UBU_BASE.
#   Exit codes: 0 deleted (or absent), 3 refused.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
ubu_safe_rm() {
    _target=$1
    if [ -L "$_target" ]; then
        ubu_log "REFUSED: $_target is a symlink"
        return 3
    fi
    [ -e "$_target" ] || return 0
    _canon=$(ubu_canon_dir "$_target")
    [ -n "$_canon" ] || { ubu_log "REFUSED: cannot resolve $_target"; return 3; }
    case "$_canon" in
        "$UBU_BASE"/?*) ;;
        *)
            ubu_log "REFUSED: $_canon is not strictly inside $UBU_BASE"
            return 3
            ;;
    esac

    # 1) Nothing may be mounted at or below the target.
    _left=$(ubu_mounts_under "$_canon")
    if [ -n "$_left" ]; then
        ubu_log "REFUSED to delete $_canon: still mounted:"
        printf '%s\n' "$_left" | sed 's/^/[uburoot]   /' >&2
        return 3
    fi

    # 2) Bind targets must be removable with rmdir (rmdir never deletes content).
    for _d in $UBU_BIND_TARGETS; do
        _p="$_canon/$_d"
        [ -L "$_p" ] && continue
        [ -d "$_p" ] || continue
        if ! rmdir "$_p" 2>/dev/null; then
            ubu_log "REFUSED to delete $_canon: mountpoint $_p is not empty"
            ubu_log "  (it may be a live mount in another namespace or hold your files)"
            return 3
        fi
    done

    # Module directory binds (KERNEL_ACCESS) must be empty too.
    for _p in "$_canon"/usr/lib/modules/*/uburoot/*; do
        if [ ! -d "$_p" ] || [ -L "$_p" ]; then continue; fi
        if ! rmdir "$_p" 2>/dev/null; then
            ubu_log "REFUSED to delete $_canon: module mountpoint $_p is not empty"
            return 3
        fi
    done

    # 3) Markers of live kernel filesystems (sysfs and everything bound into
    #    it: configfs with the USB gadget, cgroups, bpffs...), the real /dev and
    #    the kernel-change state.
    for _m in proc/self sys/kernel sys/fs dev/block dev/binder run/uburoot; do
        if [ -e "$_canon/$_m" ]; then
            ubu_log "REFUSED to delete $_canon: $_canon/$_m exists (live mount?)"
            return 3
        fi
    done
